## Document 022: 2026年4～6月期のGDP（2次速報）結果

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年4～6月期のGDP（2次速報）結果.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-08
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 / 2

2026 年 9 月 8 日

調査レポート

2026 年 4～6 月期の GDP（2 次速報）結果

～1 次速報から小幅上方修正されたが、景気の評価に変更なし～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 9 月 8 日に内閣府が公表した 4～6 月期の実質 GDP（2 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算   
＋1.4%）と、8 月に公表された 1 次速報値の同＋0.3%（同換算＋1.1%）から小幅ながら上方修正された。緩やか
な景気回復が続いているとの評価に変わりはなく、米国とイランの対立が逆風となったが、政府の物価高対策や
代替調達の進展などが下支えとなったこともあり、当初懸念されたような物価高や供給制約が景気全体を下押し
する事態は回避された。 
実質 GDP の需要項目別に 1 次速報からの 主な修正状況をみると、 まず個人消費が前期比－0.0% から   
＋0.0%に小幅に上方修正された。他方、 住宅投資は前期比－0.5%から同－0.6%に小幅に下方修正された。
企業部門では、財務省「法人企業統計」において季節調整済みの設備投資（ソフトウェアを含む・名目）が前期比
＋1.5%と増加したことから、GDP 統計の設備投資も前期比－1.2%から同－0.9%に上方修正された。民間在庫
は前回からほとんど変わらなかった。また、政府部門では、政府最終消費が前期比＋1.6%から同＋1.7%へ小幅
に上方修正された一方、公共投資は同－0.1%から同－0.5%へ下方修正された。なお、輸出、輸入ともに小幅に
下方修正されたが、外需寄与度に大きな修正はなかった。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント） 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」

### Page 2

2 / 2

 2 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 4～6 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比＋ 0.0%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境 は改善したものの （実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋ 1.0%）、イラン情勢の
悪化による消費者マインドの弱さもあり、 前期から横ばいとなった 。制度変更や価格上昇前の駆
け込みが一部に生じた影響もあって、 耐久財（前期比＋ 4.0% ）や半耐久財（同 ＋0.8% ）が増加
した一方、 非耐久財（ 同－1.9%）が加熱式たばこの値上げもあって落ち込んだほか、サービス
（同－0. 2%）も減少した。  
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－ 0.6%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数は 2 四半期ぶりに増加したものの、床面積ベースでは伸び
悩んだこともあり 、住宅投資は 3 四半期ぶりに減少 した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比－ 0.9% ） 
 企業の投資意欲は良好なものの、建設 費をはじめ種々の 投資コスト が増加していることもあって 、
設備投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。  
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋ 0.3%ポイント ） 
 2 月末からのイラン情勢悪化による供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫 など
の積み増しを進めたことから、民間在庫はプラス 寄与となった。  
 なお、公的在庫は政府が石油備蓄の放出を進めたことから、前期比寄与度－ 0.5% ポイントとな
った。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比＋1.7% ） 
 高校授業料無償化の拡充や小学校給食費の 無償化などもあり、 前期比で増加が続いた。  
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－ 0.5%） 
 名目では増加したものの、 工事コストの増加を受けた デフレーターの上昇に追いつ いておらず、
2 四半期ぶりに減少した 。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度＋ 0.5%ポイント 、実質輸出：前期比＋ 0.4%、実質輸入：同－ 1.7%） 
 輸出は前期比＋ 0.4%と増加した。財はイラン情勢の悪化を受けて中東向けを中心に減少した
一方、サービスは研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れたこともあって増加した。  
 輸入は前期比－ 1.7%と減少した。財は鉱物性燃料を中心に減少し、サービスもこのところ増加
が続いていた反動もあって減少した。  
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は＋0.5% ポイントとプラスになった。

## Document 034: 2026年8月经济数据点评：工业生产边际回升，消费、投资端数据均有所回落

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年8月经济数据点评：工业生产边际回升，消费、投资端数据均有所回落.pdf
Report source: 开源证券
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事件点评 
请务必参阅正文后面的信息披露和法律声明 1 / 7

2026 年 09 月 17 日

《社融新增不及预期，政策发力下社
融有望筑底—2026 年 8 月金融数据点
评》-2026.9.16 
《长安资管落子西咸，化债破局修复
可期—固收专题》-2026.9.15 
《美联储加息预期抬升，黄金行业信
用走势将分化—事件点评》-2026.9.14  
工业生产边际回升，消费、投资端数据均有所回落 
——2026 年 8 月经济数据点评

陈曦（分析师） 王帅中（联系人）  
chenxi2@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790521100002 
wangshuaizhong@kysec.cn 
证书编号：S0790125070016

事件：国家统计局公布 2026 年 8 月经济数据，8 月规上工增同比增长 5.2%（前
值为+4.5%，下同） ，8 月环比增长 0.54%（+0.11%） ；社零8 月同比增长 0.4%
（+0.6%） ， 固投累计同比下降7.2%（-6.7%） ， 固投(扣除房地产开发投资)累计同
比下降 4.2%（-3.7%），8 月生产端数据有所回升，消费、 投资数据较 7 月仍有所
回落。 
⚫ 8 月经济数据关注点 
生产端数据有所回升，8 月工业增加值超预期增长。8 月规上工增同比增长 5.2%，
较前值提升 0.7pct，Wind 统计的 15 家机构预测中位数为 +4.7%，平均数为
+4.7%；环比增长 0.54%，较前值提升 0.43pct。规上工增当月同比增长超预期，
边际变化上也有所回暖， 一方面与8 月以来极端天气相较于 7 月有所减少， 高温
天气有所缓解，天气对开工的扰动逐渐消退，另一方面也与 AI 链产业发展的背
景下，出口端持续保持高景气有关，出口高增仍是推动工业生产的最主要因素，
8 月进出口数据均保持高位且有所回升， 中国商品的高性价比与国际竞争力不断
增强，1-8 月规上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 10.0%，较前值增长 0.2pct，持
续刷新年内最高值，8 月规模以上工业企业实现出口交货值 1.4 万亿元，同比增
长 11.1%，保持两位数增长的同时较前值走高，其中装备制造业支撑作用明显，
出口交货值增长 12.3%， 对全部规模以上工业出口交货值增长的贡献率达81.5%。 
消费数据持续回落，出口数据回升且延续强势增长，内需弱于外需，二者数据
仍呈明显分化态势。8 月社零同比增长 0.4%， 较前值下降0.2pct ， 数据持续回落，
Wind 统计的 15 家机构预测中位数为+0.8%， 平均数为+0.7% 。 8 月社零同比回落
且不及预期值，内需偏弱的现状未能缓解，6 月随着基数扰动逐渐消退，“6·18”
促销错位推动 6 月社零超预期增长并重回正区间后， 社融增速连续两个月超预期
回落， 反映当前内需存在较大压力， 居民收入有待修复， 消费信心有待提升，从
8 月金融数据也可以看出，居民端长短贷款均有所减少，居民端持续去杠杆，内
需不足， 供强需弱的的问题进一步凸显， 内需动能有待修复。 外需火热形成鲜明
对比，8 月出口数据回升且延续强势增长， 同比增长25.00%， 较前值提升1.1pct，
从出口金额来看，仍处历史高位。 
投资增速持续下降，扣除房地产开发投资后累计同比仍处负区间，边际上下降
速度有所放缓，一线城市商品住宅销售价格环比总体上涨。1-8 月固定资产投资
累计同比下降 7.2%， 较前值下降0.5pct， 投资增速持续回落， 速度有所放缓； 扣
除房地产开发投资后累计同比下降 4.2%， 较前值下降0.5pct， 仍处负区间。 投资
增速放缓或主要由于政策支持与项目资金投放前置，一定程度上透支后续项目，
导致投资端增势显著放缓，同时国内有效需求不足进一步压制投资。1-8 月房地
产开发投资同比下降 19.9%，较前值下降 0.7pct，仍对投资有所拖累。8 月一线
城市新建商品住宅销售价格环比由 7 月持平转为上涨 0.1%，二手住宅销售价格
环比上涨 0.1%， 价格环比均仍保持上涨。8 月经济整体仍呈现供强需弱特征， 总
量与结构上的压力仍存， 往后看一方面7 月底中央政治局会议中指出宏观政策要
发力提效，加快财政支出和债券资金使用进度；另一方面 8000 亿元新型政策性
金融工具落地，发改委将部署加快推进 2026 年新型政策性金融工具投放工作，
加大对民间投资项目支持力度，支持政策的提速落地或将对后续经济形成支撑。 
⚫ 债市观点：在债市“资产荒”充分演绎后，应警惕债市调整风险 
2026 年上半年“资产荒”持续演绎，主要有三大因素驱动，资金利率持续下行、
非银久期处于低位、基本面数据走弱支撑债市走强。 
目前三大利多已经出现边际变化，警惕债市调整风险。 
资金面层面，4 月后资金利率持续回升，6 月以来 DR001 中枢稳定在 1.4%左右，
回归 2025 年下半年水平，市场宽松预期证伪；拥挤度层面，当前公募等非银机
构债券久期已达近年高位，存在拥挤度调整、估值回调风险； 
基本面层面，6 月起政策边际加码、油价冲击消退，工业数据回暖，消费同比数
据也或将随高基数效应消退逐步修复， 基本面由债市利多转为调整。此外， 市场
主流的“资产荒支撑债牛”逻辑或不成立： 贷款疲软是长期的新常态，且本轮债市
上涨，由非银抱团买入驱动，并非银行缺资产导致的配置行为。 
整体来看，在债市“资产荒”已经充分演绎之后，特别是如果降息预期发酵 /或者
落地，应警惕债市调整风险。 
⚫ 风险提示：政策变化超预期；经济变化超预期。

《证券期货投资者适当性管理办法》 、 《证券经营机构投资者适当性管理实施指引（试行） 》已于2017年7月1日起正
式实施。根据上述规定，开源证券评定此研报的风险等级为R3（中风险） ，因此通过公共平台推送的研报其适用的
投资者类别仅限定为专业投资者及风险承受能力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者。若您并非专业投资者及风险承受能
力为C3、C4、C5的普通投资者，请取消阅读，请勿收藏、接收或使用本研报中的任何信息。
因此受限于访问权限的设置，若给您造成不便，烦请见谅！感谢您给予的理解与配合。
分析师承诺
本研究报告的署名人员具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格或相当的专业胜任能力， 以勤勉的职业态
度，独立、客观地出具本报告，并对内容和观点负责。本报告清晰准确地反映了署名人员的研究观点，所包含的分
析基于各种假设， 不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。 本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型均有其局限性， 估
值结果不保证所涉及证券能够在该价格交易。 本报告署名人员保证他们报酬的任何一部分不曾与， 不与， 也将不会

与本报告中具体的推荐意见或观点有直接或间接的联系。
股票投资评级说明
评级 说明
证券评级
买入（Buy） 预计相对强于市场表现 20%以上；
增持（outperform） 预计相对强于市场表现 5%～20%；
中性（Neutral） 预计相对市场表现在－5%～＋5%之间波动；
减持（underperform） 预计相对弱于市场表现 5%以下。
行业评级
看好（overweight） 预计行业超越整体市场表现；
中性（Neutral） 预计行业与整体市场表现基本持平；
看淡（underperform） 预计行业弱于整体市场表现。
备注：评级标准为以报告日后的 6~12 个月内，证券相对于市场基准指数的涨跌幅表现，其中 A 股基准指数为沪
深 300 指数、港股基准指数为恒生指数、新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针
对做市转让标的） 、 美股基准指数为标普500 或纳斯达克综合指数。我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同
的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决

定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较
完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。
分析、估值方法的局限性说明
本报告所包含的分析基于各种假设， 不同假设可能导致分析结果出现重大不同。 本报告采用的各种估值方法及模型
均有其局限性，估值结果不保证所涉及证券能够在该价格交易。

## Document 203: ACCI-Westpac Business Survey Q3 2026 (2026-09-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/ACCI-Westpac Business Survey Q3 2026 (2026-09-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ACCI–Westpac
Survey of Industrial Trends
Australian Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking Corporation
260th report September 2026 (survey conducted from 20 July to 3 September 2026)
 Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained sluggish through Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI
Actual Composite edging slightly lower from 50.5 in Q2 to 49.1 in Q3, consistent with a neutral-to-slightly-
contractionary reading.
 Manufacturers are contending with numerous headwinds at present. As the Middle East conflict has continued

to unfold, the combination of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering uncertainty has made for a
more challenging environment for consumer demand and some parts of the construction sector.
 Growth in new orders remained absent in Q3, with a net 2% of respondents reporting a decrease after only a
net 1% reported an increase in Q2. Sluggish demand saw a similar net percentage of manufacturers wind back
production, reduce headcount and decrease overtime. Manufacturers instead chipped away at existing order

backlogs and built up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
 Spillovers from the Middle East conflict continue to play a significant role when it comes to cost pressures.
A net 48% of respondents reported an increase in average unit costs during Q3, barely changed from the net
51% reporting an increase in Q2. Pressures remain particularly acute in sub-sectors with more direct exposure
to fuel, fertiliser and other supply chain stresses, including machinery and equipment, fabricated and primary

metals, chemicals, wood and furniture.
 Despite persistent cost pressures, the proportion of firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell to a
net 24% in Q3. This remained well below the proportion reporting higher average unit costs, suggesting
manufacturers continue to absorb part of the increase in costs rather than fully passing them on to customers,
placing pressure on margins.
 The Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved confidence

in future activity. Stronger expectations for new orders and output suggest manufacturers expect the current
period of soft demand to be relatively short lived.
 However, while manufacturers may see some light at the end of the tunnel for demand and production, they
do not see a significant let-up in cost pressures anytime soon, with a net 43% anticipating a further increase
in average unit costs over the next three months. Ongoing margin squeeze is the baseline view among

manufacturers, contributing to a weaker outlook for profit growth.
 Manufacturers remain somewhat cautious about their investment plans for the year ahead. On balance, a net
14% of firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over the next twelve months, while a net 4%
intend to increase building investment. While expectations have turned more bullish in some areas, until such
outcomes materialise, some level of hesitancy may persist.

The Survey of Industrial Trends produced by the Australian
Chamber of Commerce and Industry & Westpac Banking
Corporation is a quarterly publication.
It is Australia's longest running business survey dating
from 1966, providing a timely update on manufacturing and

insights into economy-wide trends.

Net response or "balance" is calculated by the proportion of
"ups" less "downs" on individual questions, thereby yielding
the net balance. A positive balance indicates a net upward
or improving trend and a minus balance a net downward or
deteriorating trend.

The 260th consecutive survey was closed on Monday, x
September 2026 A total of 413 responses were received,
and provided a reasonable cross–section of Australian
manufacturing in respect of industry groups and size of
operation.

3
Key Survey Results
September 2026
• Manufacturing sentiment about the general business
outlook deteriorated sharply last quarter, as manufacturers
were confronted with acute cost pressures and a stalling in
new orders growth.
• Sentiment remains pessimistic but conditions improved

in Q3, with a net 12% of respondents expect the general
business situation to deteriorate over the next six months,
compared to a net 21% anticipating a deterioration in Q2.
• While manufacturers are expecting an improvement in
demand and production near-term, the backdrop of rising
costs, higher interest rates and difficulties in sourcing
labour will continue to present challenges for businesses.
• Momentum in Australia's manufacturing sector remained

sluggish in Q3, with the Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite
edging slightly lower from 50.5 to 49.1, consistent with a
neutral-to-slightly-contractionary reading.
• Underpinning the result were small declines across all of
the constituent components: output, new orders, overtime,
numbers employed and order backlogs.
• In contrast, the Westpac-ACCI Expected Composite
rose from 52.7 in Q2 to 57.9 in Q3, indicating improved
confidence in future activity. However, recent history has

shown that expectations can run ahead of actual future
outcomes for a period of time, and that could remain the
case should current headwinds persist.
• The Labour Market Composite held steady moving into the
second half of the year, nudging only slightly lower from
44.7 in Q2 to 44.5 in Q3.
• Results in the Labour Market Composite have foreshadowed
the trends in official labour market data for the market
sector, where jobs growth started to recover last year and

kick into a higher gear earlier this year.
• More recently, the Labour Market Composite has pointed to
a renewed slowing in jobs growth momentum. This would
be consistent with a moderation in labour demand over the
coming quarters as the effects of slower economic growth
from high inflation and interest rates continue to weigh on
the activity.

4
Manufacturing & the Business Cycle
• The Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite has a solid track
record as a timely indicator of near-term domestic economic
conditions and turning points in the cycle.
• The Q2 National Accounts showed that growth is slowing
but not as rapidly as initially feared. Underneath that is a

complex mix of headwinds and tailwinds. There are clear
drags coming from higher interest rates, elevated inflation,
slowing public investment and the housing market (albeit
so far confined to the direct effects from lower housing
turnover). However, these are being offset by tailwinds
related to firmer business investment, particularly in data
centres, energy and related infrastructure.
• This survey suggests softer demand and lingering cost
pressures remain a persistent themes that will continue to

shape momentum in local manufacturing and the domestic
economy over the coming months.
The Business Cycle & Economic Outlook
Manufacturing & Business Investment
• The ACCI-Westpac survey has historically provided a broad
gauge on equipment spending trends in the manufacturing
sector. However, actual spending has persistently under
performed the investment intentions in this survey,
suggesting that some plans are failing to materialise.
• Official data had been turning more constructive, but

momentum looks to have slipped once again. The National
Accounts showed that an investment pulse is underway in
Australia, but it is being chiefly driven by a few sectors –
particularly data centres and renewables. Outside of these
sectors, growth in equipment spending is more subdued.
• Ongoing uncertainty surrounding the Middle East conflict,
together with elevated cost pressures, higher interest
rates and a subdued demand outlook are likely to remain a

headwind for investment.
Australian & World Manufacturing Surveys
• Global and Australian manufacturing cycles tend to be
synchronised, especially at major turning points. The
Westpac-ACCI Actual Composite typically moves in line
with global manufacturing conditions, albeit with some
level of variation within any given cycle.
• However, more recently, trends have started to diverge
across the major manufacturing hubs. The US continues to
weather the headwinds associated with tariff uncertainty

and the US-Iran war, with the ISM PMI at 54.6 in August,
firmly in expansionary territory. It is a similar story in
Europe, where the S&P Global PMI has risen to 52.7 in
August, its highest in many years. Meanwhile, China's
manufacturing remains relatively lacklustre, similar to
Australia, with the NBS PMI around breakeven territory at
49.8 as of August.

Activity & Orders
• Growth in new orders remained sluggish in Q3, with a net
2% of respondents reporting a decrease, easing from a net
1% reporting an increase in Q2.
• As the Middle East conflict continues, the combination
of elevated inflation, higher interest rates and lingering

uncertainty has made for a more challenging environment
for consumer demand and some parts of the construction
sector.
• Manufacturers expect growth in new orders to improve
over the next three months, with a net 20% anticipating an
increase. Whether these expectations will be met given the
challenging backdrop is a key question.

Exports
Q2 2026 Q3 2026
Actual – net balance 9 8
Expected – net balance 11 12
• Growth in manufacturing exports were broadly stable
moving into the second half of the year, with a net 8% of
firms reporting an increase in Q3, following Q2's net 9%.
• Demand from our Asian trading partners have continued to
support processed food and agriculture exports. However,
a firmer AUD and uncertainty from the Middle East conflict

would have dampened growth in the quarter. US tariff risks
meanwhile remain a wildcard, although fortunately, it has
had little overall impact to date.
• Expectations have cooled compared to earlier in the year
but they remain positive, with a net 12% expecting an
increase in exports over the next three months.
• After slowing materially last quarter, output took another
step lower in Q3. A net 2% of firms reported a decrease in
output, the first decline in a year.

• Demand remained sluggish during the quarter, leading
manufacturers to chip away at existing order backlogs and
build up some inventory in anticipation of future increases.
• Looking ahead, a net 26% of firms expect an increase in
output over the next three months, suggesting the current
soft patch is expected to only be temporary. However, it is
important to note that expectations have been more bullish
than actual future outcomes over the past two years.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
• Manufacturers turned more cautious about their investment
plans for the year ahead, following the onset of the Middle
East conflict.
• That broadly remained the case in Q3, though investment
intentions were still positive. On balance, a net 14% of
firms intend to increase plant and equipment spending over

the next twelve months, while a net 4% intend to increase
building investment.
• There remains a significant degree of uncertainty around
the economic outlook. Expectations for production and
orders have turned more bullish, but until such outcomes
materialise, some level of hesitancy might persist.
Investment & Profitability
• Profit expectations took a substantial hit last quarter,
reflecting a combination of surging cost pressures, higher
interest rates and the onset of the Middle East conflict.

This saw a sharp increase in cost pressures and substantial
uncertainty around the outlook.
• With these headwinds persisting, the outlook for profits
was little changed. Only a net 10% of firms expect an
improvement in profits over the coming year, below the
historic average of a net 19%.
• While manufacturers are anticipating an improvement in
new orders over the coming months, cost pressures are
also expected to remain acute, compressing margins.

• Capacity utilisation has quickly swung from a period of
overutilisation earlier this year, prior to the onset of the
Middle East conflict, back toward underutilisation.
• In the latest survey, fewer firms reported operating at
above average capacity (23% to 18%) with more firms
reporting that they were operating at below average
capacity (25% to 28%). This saw an increase in the net
balance of firms reporting capacity underutilisation, up from
2% in Q2 to 10% in Q3.

• This is still a fairly robust result. Over the full history
of the survey, manufacturers have tended to report a
larger degree of underutilisation, especially during cycles
characterised by slower growth and/or higher interest
rates.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
• Employment dynamics in the manufacturing sector remain
sluggish. A net 2% of firms reported a decline in the size
of the workforce in Q3, following on from a net 4% also
reporting a pull-back in Q2.
• Manufacturers were confronted with a stagnation in new
orders growth and pull-back in production during the

quarter, providing little scope to increase headcount.
• Over the next three months however, a net 7% of
firms expect to increase headcount. While modest, the
expected lift in employment coincides with manufacturers'
expectations for a rebound in new orders and production
over the coming months.
The Labour Market
• Historically, firms' views on the difficulty of finding labour
tracks shifts in the Australian unemployment rate, albeit
with some variation between cycles depending on the

industry mix of employment growth.
• In Q3, a larger share of manufacturers reported difficulty
in sourcing labour, with a net 28.3% indicating labour was
"harder to find" compared to three months ago.
• Overall jobs growth has held up well so far this year, but
the spill overs from the Middle East conflict and recent
interest rate rises are expected to weigh on jobs growth
over the coming months.
• Manufacturers' use of overtime deteriorated further in Q3,

with a net 3% reporting a decline, in line with expectations
from the prior quarter.
• Hours worked usually adjusts more quickly to shifts in
demand conditions than employment. The fact that both
eased materially at the same time points to a more abrupt
cooling in labour demand within the sector.
• Manufacturers anticipate a modest improvement in
overtime usage over the coming three months, with a net
5% expecting an increase. If manufacturers' expectations

for demand crystallise, some increase in overtime would be
required to avoid building up order backlogs.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
8
• Despite persistent unit cost pressures, the proportion of
firms reporting an increase in selling prices fell slightly
during Q3, to a net 24%.
• This was well below the net 48% reporting an increase in
average unit costs over the same period. It also fell well
short of manufacturers' own expectations from last quarter,

implying margins are being squeezed as firms only partially
pass through higher costs onto customers.
• Manufacturers expect only partial pass-through of higher
costs to customers will remain the case over the next
few months, with a net 27% expecting to increase selling
prices.
Prices & Inflation
• Results around wage expectations in the manufacturing
sector were broadly unchanged versus last quarter, with a
net 31% indicating they expect their next enterprise wage

agreement to delivery an outcome above the last.
• The survey suggests no real step-change in expectations
following the Fair Work Commission's 2026 Annual Wage
Review, which announced a larger-than-expected 4.75%
increase in award wages in Q2, to be implemented during
Q3. Expectations are still elevated, and this may compound
existing non-labour cost pressures for many manufacturers.
• Consistent with this survey, official ABS data points to a
turnaround in the wages growth trend, up to 3.3%yr in Q2,

slightly outstripping the national average of 3.2%yr.
• Manufacturers are grappling with acute cost pressures as
the supply shock stemming from the Middle East continues
to impact. In Q3, a net 48% of firms reported a rise in
average unit costs.
• Sub-sectors with more direct exposure to fuel, energy
intensive goods such as fertilisers, plastics and metals are
reporting more acute pressure, including machinery and
equipment, fabricated and primary metals, chemicals, wood

and furniture.
• Manufacturers do not see a significant let-up in cost
pressures near-term, with a net 43% anticipating a further
increase in average unit costs over the next three months.

Factors Limiting Production

• Orders remain an important constraint on production – a

lack of demand saw 36% of respondents cite this as the
single factor most limiting production. However, this is still
well below historical averages, showcasing the relative
importance of other supply-side limitations currently.
• Labour remains a clear example, with 20% citing it as the
primary constraint in Q3, above Q2's 17% and well above
the pre-pandemic average. Difficulties in sourcing labour
remains a persistent theme.
• Material shortages appear to have eased, with the

proportion of respondents citing it as the most limiting
factor down from 14% to 8%. While off its recent highs, it
is still well above the pre-pandemic average.
• Capacity dipped slightly to 8% in Q3, but the proportion
of respondents citing None has steadily risen over recent
quarters, up to 11% in Q3, suggesting that despite recent
disruptions and volatility, a decent portion of manufacturers'
production is returning back to some kind of 'norm'.

The Westpac–ACCI Composite Indices
The Westpac–ACCI Actual and Expected Composite indices are weighted averages of the activity measures in the survey. The 
weights are as follows: employment 20%; new orders 30%; output 25%; order backlog 15%; overtime 10% .
The Westpac–ACCI Labour Market Composite is a function of actual employment, with a weight of almost 50%, as well as: 
expected employment; expected overtime; new orders; order backlog; and expected order backlog.

## Document 215: Anthropic、OpenAI、谷歌等AI巨头遭反垄断诉讼，被指协同放缓技术发展

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic、OpenAI、谷歌等AI巨头遭反垄断诉讼，被指协同放缓技术发展.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

呼吁 “AI 减速 ” 反成垄断靶⼦。 OpenAI 、⾕歌等四⼤巨头突遭起诉，其放缓 AI 发展的安全共
识被控涉嫌⾮法商业合谋。原告痛批巨头借安全之名私定规则，主张监管权应归属政府。
此案⼀旦发酵，恐深远重塑全球 AI 竞争格局。
摘要：
⼈⼯智能⾏业的监管争议正从政策层⾯蔓延⾄法律战场。
据 Politico 报道，四家原告于上周五在加利福尼亚州联邦法院提起⺠事诉讼，指控 Anthropic 、
OpenAI 、 SpaceXAI 及⾕歌涉嫌违反美国反垄断法，通过公开协调放缓 AI 发展速度的⽅式实施⾮
法商业合谋。上述四家公司均未⽴即回应置评请求。
诉讼的直接导⽕索是 Anthropic ⾸席执⾏官 Dario Amodei 本⽉早些时候发表的⼀篇⽂章。⽂章呼
吁 " 全⾏业协调 " 以 " 控制 AI 前沿发展节奏 " ，随后获得 SpaceXAI 负责⼈埃隆 · ⻢斯克、 OpenAI ⾸席
执⾏官 Sam Altman 及⾕歌 DeepMind 联合创始⼈ Demis Hassabis 的公开附议。原告⽅认为，这

⼀系列表态构成竞争对⼿之间的⾮法商业协议。此案⼀旦进⼊实质审理阶段，将对 AI ⾏业的竞争
格局及监管⾛向产⽣深远影响。
诉讼核⼼指控：公开表态被认定为⾮法协调
诉讼在加利福尼亚州北区联邦法院提起，代理律师之⼀ Nick Rowley 在声明中表⽰，提起此案旨
在防⽌ " 全球最具权势的营利性科技公司之间的私下⾃利协议 " 导致 AI" 迅速脱离⼈类控制 " 。
Rowley 在声明中称， " 当涉及核战争等灭绝性威胁，以及如今⼈类历史上最⼤⻛险时，⼈类理应
获得铁板钉钉的保障。法治应由政府以透明、合法的⽅式建⽴，并对公众负责。 "
原告⽅的法律逻辑在于， Dario Amodei 的公开呼吁及其他⾏业领袖的响应，在形式上构成竞争
对⼿之间就商业⾏为达成的协议，触碰了美国反垄断法的红线 —— 即便相关表态以公共安全为
名。
原告构成：律师与普通公⺠联合出击
四名原告背景各异。律师 Cheyenne Hunt 此前曾参与揭露前加利福尼亚州众议员 Eric Swalwell
及前缅因州参议员候选⼈ Graham Platner 性⾏为不端指控，具有⼀定公众知名度。另外两名原

告为佛罗⾥达州律师 Charles Buist 和 Nick Spetsas ，第四名原告为加利福尼亚州居⺠ Christine
Bullock 。
诉讼⽂件显⽰，原告⽅有意将此案扩展为集体诉讼，代表所有受相关公司放缓 AI 发展决策影响的
群体。这⼀意图意味着潜在索赔规模可能⼤幅扩⼤，并将进⼀步提升此案对⾏业的震慑效应。
安全争议引发减速呼声
此次诉讼的背景是 AI 安全领域持续升温的争论。据悉，现任及前任 AI 研究⼈员近期发出严厉警
告，认为若缺乏⾜够的安全护栏， AI 技术的持续发展可能对⼈类⽣存构成潜在的存在性威胁，上
述⾏业领袖的减速表态正是在此背景下相继出现。
然⽽，此次诉讼将这⼀安全讨论置于反垄断框架之下，形成了截然不同的法律叙事：原告⽅并
⾮质疑 AI 安全⻛险本⾝，⽽是主张，⽆论出于何种动机，竞争对⼿之间的协调⾏为均不应游离于
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

• Data centres, power, compute and private investment-grade financing is further
diversifying private capital from buyouts.
• But the risk of AI disruption, overbuild, and capital misallocation is a frequent risk

discussed by large, listed private capital managers
• Our spotlight section includes an analysis of AI-related overfinancing risk
• Still, the macro environment is now the top point of concern amongst PC managers, even
more so than the exit environment
• Private capital growth remains strong but is becoming increasingly K-shaped. Scale,
investment performance, origination capabilities and product breadth are driving
fundraising toward the largest platforms, while weaker managers and less differentiated

products face pressure.
• Fundraising, AUM and fee-related earnings generally reached record or near-record
levels, despite weak sponsor M&A, uneven exits and disruption in parts of private wealth
and software.
• There has also been a drop in the number of articles on private credit in the media,
especially when it comes to “crisis”-mentioning variety. With public BDCs being at the
centre of this year’s rout, the drop in such trackable indicators likely bodes well for

sentiment and thus flows.
• The overall tone of Q2 results was strongly positive and confident, especially on
fundraising and structural growth. However, management teams were more cautious on
the timing of monetisations, private-wealth recovery, AI capacity risks and software
disruption.

### Page 4

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
3
• Private capital growth remains strong but is becoming
increasingly K-shaped. Scale, investment performance,
origination capabilities and product breadth are driving
fundraising toward the largest platforms, while weaker
managers and less differentiated products face pressure.
• Fundraising, AUM and fee-related earnings generally
reached record or near-record levels, despite weak

sponsor M&A, uneven exits and disruption in parts of
private wealth and software.
• The overall tone was strongly positive and confident,
especially on fundraising and structural growth.
However, management teams were more cautious on the
timing of monetisations, private-wealth recovery, AI
capacity risks and software disruption.
• AI is creating a need for data centres, power, compute
and private investment-grade financing that further
diversifies private capital from buyouts.

• Amongst the concerns we track, managers were relatively
more concerned about the macro environment and AI
effects, including the risk of overbuild in related
infrastructure.

### Page 7

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
6
Key themes from Q2 earnings calls
Large, listed managers talk AI, wealth, fundraising, exits and credit
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank.
1. AI infrastructure has become a firm-wide capital deployment and earnings theme, extending beyond data centres into power, compute, chips, connectivity and investment-
grade financing. Power availability is emerging as a more important constraint than capital. Although concentrated exposure to AI infrastructure is a risk if demand

expectations change, managers stressed contracted revenues, strong counterparties and hard-asset collateral as mitigants.
• Blackstone’s data-centre platform reached $185bn of total value, including projects under construction, up from $130bn at the start of 2026. Management said the
platform could double over the next few years and that the market for long-term ownership of stabilised data centres could eventually reach $1tn. The firm also said
nine of its ten largest Q2 mark-ups were AI-related.

• Apollo cited more than $8tn of cumulative AI-infrastructure investment through the cycle and led a $35bn Broadcom financing, described as the largest private-credit
financing to date.
• Brookfield estimated $10tn of AI-related capex across energy, data centres, compute and adjacent infrastructure. The firm said AI-related deployment was probably
running at approximately twice its normal 15%-20% business growth rate.
• Blue Owl reported more than 140 data centres owned or under construction and 15.3GW of leased and owned capacity

2. Private wealth remains strategically important, but private credit experienced a confidence and liquidity test. Early Q3 data suggests stabilising fundraising. The better wealth
outcomes increasingly came from private equity, infrastructure, real estate and multi-asset products rather than traditional private credit. Earnings calls suggest some
international family offices and smaller institutions behaved more cyclically than core US individual investors.

3. Record fundraising despite a "noisy" backdrop. Fundraising is concentrating with scaled, diversified platforms.
4. Realisations are recovering, but the market is highly selective (see pages 17-21 of this report)
5. Credit is broadening beyond sponsor-backed direct lending and real assets, infrastructure, defence and secondaries are benefiting from secular demand
6. Earnings are shifting toward recurring fees, transaction revenues and fee-related performance revenues. Firms are becoming more effective at capturing economics from

origination, financing and perpetual vehicles amidst a strategic shift from simply managing third-party capital toward monetising the broader platform through origination,
syndication, financing and advisory services.

### Page 8

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
7
Key risks from Q2 earnings calls
Aside from AI, concerns are rising about rates and credit
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank.
1. AI overbuild, capital misallocation and execution amongst most consistently voiced risks
• Brookfield said concerns about excess infrastructure capacity were reasonable and that, as in railways and fibre, some capita l would inevitably be poorly allocated.

• Blackstone acknowledged the potential for excessive exuberance, despite its conviction that power, chips and entitlements con strain supply.
• Both firms also identified scarce inputs and complex development requirements as execution bottlenecks.
2. AI disruption to software and white-collar services
• Blackstone said software, professional services and information services were experiencing weaker buyer interest despite many companies continuing to perform operationally.

• TPG reported mid-teens software bookings growth and said over 75% of its software exposure was well positioned to benefit from AI.
• Blue Owl said it had gained increasing comfort around software transition risk but continued to recognise it as a valid issue
3. Wealth-channel redemptions. Management frames this as narrative-driven, but a slower-than-expected sentiment recovery is a risk to retail flows. Managers emphasised that requests were
concentrated among a minority of investors concentrated in non-US family offices and smaller institutions and that underlying portfolio performance remained sound.

• The recovery appears to be under way but remains gradual. Blue Owl explicitly rejected a V -shaped recovery assumption, while Ares estimated that its BDC could return to flow stasis over the
next two to three quarters if current trends continued.
4. Realisation timing and reliance on capital markets. Managers stressed that timing is difficult to predict.
• TPG expects transaction and monitoring fees to step down in Q3 after pulling forward some transactions into Q2. Carlyle simil arly said Q2 capital-markets activity was unlikely to repeat in Q3

or Q4. Blackstone expects lower Q3 realisations before a stronger Q4 and 2027.
5. Credit-cycle normalisation and growing dispersion remain risks, although managers generally reported sound portfolio fundamental s in Q2.
6. Insurance competition, spread compression and regulation
• KKR said competition had increased and it was allocating somewhat less capital to insurance at current return levels. Managem ent described sector ROEs as structurally low in the current

competitive environment. Apollo cited 36 asset-manager entrants into retail annuities and argued that some competitors lack matu re origination, liability-generation and operating
capabilities. Apollo also highlighted offshore regulatory arbitrage and pushed for “equal capital for equal risk”, warning th at weaker regulatory structures could undermine industry trust.
7. Rates, inflation, energy and geopolitics. The macro backdrop remained a recurring source of uncertainty:

• Carlyle cited war in the Middle East, pressure on energy markets, stubborn inflation and AI -related public-market volatility.
• TPG said geopolitical uncertainty and changing rate expectations had reduced private -equity activity.
• Blackstone said higher rates were delaying the real -estate recovery and had pushed some exits out.

### Page 10

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
9
Management comments – a few records in Q2 and a confident tone
Source: Company reports, Deutsche Bank.
• Stephen Schwarzman of Blackstone: “Blackstone delivered an outstanding second quarter, highlighted by strong growth in earnings and nearly $70 billion of inflows. Our
decision to lean into the artificial intelligence megatrend is leading to standout investment performance across numerous strategies and creating extraordinary opportunities for

growth. We’ve become a trusted partner at scale to many of the key innovators in this ecosystem, which positions our firm extremely well for the future.”
• Joseph Bae and Scott Nuttall of KKR: “Our second quarter results reflect the strength and breadth of KKR's global franchise. We reported record Fee Related Earnings, Total
Operating Earnings and Adjusted Net Income per share on both a quarterly and trailing twelve-month basis. Our continued return of capital to clients led to our strongest

monetization quarter ever and helped drive record new capital inflows over the past 12 months.”
• Marc Rowan of Apollo: “Our strong second quarter results reflect record earnings across Asset Management and Retirement Services, highlighting the quality and growing
scale of our business. We are at the forefront of modernizing how private markets operate by enhancing transparency, improving liquidity, and broadening access. In a market
evolving quickly with increasing demand for capital, the breadth of our origination capabilities combined with a principal mindset positions us to help shape what comes next.”

• Harvey Schwartz of Carlyle: “The second quarter was one of Carlyle’s strongest quarters in recent years, underscoring the power of our diversified platform. We delivered record
Fee Related Earnings, our highest Distributable Earnings in nearly four years, alongside strong fundraising, and exceptional realization activity. Carlyle continues to distinguish
itself as an industry leader and an outlier in returning capital to our clients, distributing nearly $7 billion during the quarter and $37 billion over the past year. …”

• Michael Arougheti of Ares: “We generated strong second-quarter results, including another record quarter of fundraising with more than $36 billion of inflows as our clients
continue to reward us due to our strong and consistent fund performance across our strategies. Our diverse global originationplatform enabled us to remain active investing in
attractive opportunities across the platform in a slower transaction environment and we are now seeing a meaningful pickup inour firmwide investment pipeline.”

• Jon Winkelried of TPG: “TPG delivered record results during the first half of 2026, reflecting the strength of our diversified and growing franchise…As clients increasingly
partner with TPG for access to our differentiated offerings across strategies, we continue to see significant opportunities to further expand our platform and build long-term
value for our shareholders.“
• Doug Ostrover and Marc Lipschultz of Blue Owl: “Blue Owl's ongoing growth in the second quarter reflects the strong investment performance we continue to generate across

strategies and the diversification of our business across platforms and geographies… The results we reported this morning continue to demonstrate the resilience of our
business and highlight our positioning as a key provider of scaled capital solutions in this evolving market landscape."

There has also been a drop in the number of articles on private credit in the media, especially when it comes to
“crisis”-mentioning variety. With public BDCs being at the centre of this year’s rout, the drop in such trackable
indicators likely bodes well for sentiment and thus flows.

### Page 18

Luke Templeman | Galina Pozdnyakova | Private capital monitor: AI is the 2nd biggest risk  |  September 2026
17
Realisations 1/5
Reopening IPO markets, AI-related corporate demand and mature portfolios are all catalysts. But timing remains
unpredictable and exposed to geopolitics, public-market volatility, rates and AI-related valuation uncertainty.
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank.
1. KKR described Q2 as the largest monetisation quarter in its history, with realised

performance income of $848m and realised investment income of $220m.
Remaining unrealised gains were still approximately $18bn.
2. Carlyle returned nearly $7bn to investors in Q2 and $37bn over the prior year. Its
US buyout strategy returned 23% of fair value over 12 months, which
management said was more than twice the current industry average and above
the 20% long-term industry average.
3. TPG generated $5bn of Q2 realisations and almost $14bn in H1, up 28% year over

year.
4. Blackstone outlined the following segmentation when it comes to investor
demand for assets:
• AI, power, electrification and energy assets: strong strategic, sponsor and
IPO demand
• AI-unaffected businesses: broadly available liquidity
• Software, professional services and information services: materially weaker
activity because of AI uncertainty.
The firm estimated the third category represents approximately 30%-40% of the
private-equity market.

5. TPG similarly noted that industry realisations were down about 46% quarter over
quarter, although its own H1 activity was higher. It expects its monetisation
cadence to accelerate toward year-end and into 2027. Brookfield expects
transaction volumes to build in H2, while Blackstone expects weaker Q3
realisations followed by a stronger Q4 and 2027.

18
Realisations 2/5
More AI-related businesses are likely to come to the market even after the big AI firms go public. Tech IPOs have been relatively muted
despite the boom in financing this decade. Investors’ appetite for AI and big capital markets issuances may push more manager s to exit
tech holdings while the mood lasts. There have been just c $7bn in pure-play AI IPOs in the US since 2025.

Source: Dealogic, OECD, Deutsche Bank.

## Document 320: Riksbank preview_ Hold now, hike in November

Source PDF: all report pdf/Riksbank preview_ Hold now, hike in November.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 18 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  18 September 2026
Riksbank preview: Hold now, hike in
November
A surprise hike shouldn’t be entirely ruled out on 24 September, but we
favour a hawkish hold instead, with strong hints that tightening is
coming. We expect a hike at the November meeting, followed by at least
another one by end-1Q27. We have revised our EUR/SEK profile higher
on the back of our new Fed and ECB calls, but SEK appreciation remains

our baseline
The Riksbank has been on hold despite energy price increases
Consensus is in favour of the Riksbank staying on hold at 1.75% on 24 September. We agree:
August inflation undershot expectations and does not call for immediate action. Still, external
pressure is mounting, and a surprise hike cannot be fully ruled out. Higher oil prices and the
more proactive stance of the European Central Bank and Federal Reserve could lead the
Riksbank to bring forward a hike that already looked likely early next year, as inflation

gradually moves back above target.
We are therefore revising our call and now expect a 25bp hike in November or December,
with a preference for November. We do not expect back-to-back moves, with the following

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 2
hike in February or March.
Inflation muted, but should rise
Swedish inflation is not easy to measure this year. The Riksbank has acknowledged the
dampening effect of temporary tax measures and shifted its focus towards more core
measures. As shown below, the headline tax-adjusted inflation is slightly above target but the
index excluding energy stabilised at 1.3% in July and August. These inflation dynamics are far

from alarming, and broadly in line with the Riksbank’s main scenario presented in June.
That said, the direction of travel for inflation remains higher. Accommodative policy, the likely
expiry of tax cuts this autumn, rising energy prices and a weaker krona all point to upside risks.
We expect the Riksbank to revise its inflation forecasts higher at the September meeting. In
particular, a core inflation forecast above the current 2.0% for 2027 could be read as an

implicit endorsement of market pricing for multiple rate hikes.
Sweden's inflation has been dampened by tax effects
Source: ING, SCB
Strong growth in 2Q, slower in 3Q
Second quarter growth surprised to the upside, with annual growth reaching 3.3%, well above
the Riksbank’s 1.9% forecast. Strong fixed investment, household consumption and net exports
were the main drivers.
However, GDP contracted by 0.8% month-on-month in July, suggesting momentum weakened

at the start of the third quarter. Meanwhile, the unemployment rate rose sharply to 8.9% in
August, although the Riksbank is likely to view these job figures with a degree of caution.
No urgency now, but November hike on the cards
Overall, we expect the Riksbank to leave rates unchanged in September while signalling that a

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 3
hike is drawing closer. A move next week cannot be fully ruled out, but with only 5bp priced
and August inflation broadly benign, the case for immediate action remains weak.
That said, the medium-term inflation outlook points in the other direction. Rising price
pressures in the coming quarters, external factors and a weaker krona could encourage the
Riksbank to act pre-emptively, particularly given the resilience of growth, paving the way for a

hike in November.
The main caveat is the situation in the Gulf, but following this meeting, a decline in oil prices
would need to be much larger to persuade the Riksbank to forgo an autumn hike.
Rate projections to send hawkish message
We expect the rate projections to be revised higher to fully reflect a year-end hike, implying a
4Q average policy rate of around 1.88%. We also expect the timing of subsequent hikes to be
brought forward, lifting the average 1Q 2027 policy rate from 1.89% to around 2.05%, before

reaching 2.2-2.4% in the second half of 2027.
While this would still fall short of market pricing for rates to reach 3.0% by end-2027, it would
represent enough of a mark-to-market adjustment to support front-end SEK rates.
Higher rates to lend some support to the krona
We think a hawkish message next week will consolidate expectations for a November move
(now 22bp priced in) and could fuel speculation about back-to-back hikes. Over the past two
weeks, both the euro and the dollar have received support from their respective central banks.

Next week, we think it will be the krona’s turn.
Still, SEK cannot be viewed through the Riksbank lens alone. EUR/SEK has been driven higher by
elevated oil prices, weaker risk sentiment and a hawkish ECB. The Riksbank can counter the
latter by narrowing the gap with ECB pricing, but lower oil prices are still required to drive a
sustained move lower in EUR/SEK.
We have also revised our ECB and Fed forecasts to include one additional hike by year-end

from each. As a result, the floor has been set higher for EUR/SEK, both directly through the ECB
and indirectly through the Fed via risk sentiment and liquidity conditions. Our baseline remains
for oil prices to decline sharply from 4Q, leaving our EUR/SEK profile downward sloping.
That said, we have raised our forecast path over the coming quarters and now see EUR/SEK at
11.10 by year-end. In the near term, another leg higher in oil prices and risk sentiment jitters

could lift EUR/SEK back to 11.40-11.50, its highest level since February 2025, even if the
Riksbank prepares for a November hike.
A lot of media attention has focused on the very close result in the Swedish election this

## Document 326: Softer Polish industry underscore risks to GDP growth outlook

Source PDF: all report pdf/Softer Polish industry underscore risks to GDP growth outlook.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 18 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  18 September 2026   POLAND
Softer Polish industry underscore risks
to GDP growth outlook
Despite the conflict in the Middle East, Poland s second-quarter GDP
growth was buoyant at 3.9% YoY – but softer-than-expected August
industrial output data, alongside the prolonged energy crisis, calls for
caution on the economic outlook for the coming quarters. We re sticking
to our conservative forecast of GDP growth at 3.4% this year

Softer industrial data for August has only increased our caution surrounding Poland's economic outlook
Industrial output short of expectations in August
Poland’s industrial output increased by 4.3% year-on-year in August (ING: 8.3%; consensus:
6.1%), following growth of 4.8% YoY in July (revised down from 5.1% YoY). Given a favourable
calendar effect in the form of one additional working day compared with a year earlier and a
low statistical base from last year, the result is disappointing.

Growth in manufacturing output slowed to 3.4% YoY from 4.2% YoY a month earlier. Energy
production continued to expand at a double-digit pace (10.5% YoY), as did mining output
(18.7% YoY). Seasonally adjusted data points to a 1.2% month-on-month decline in industrial
production, following two months of flat monthly readings.

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 2
Industrial production down in August
Industrial production MoM (SA)
Source: GUS, ING.
Growth in August was driven mainly by the production of intermediate goods (8.5% YoY), non-
durable consumer goods (4.8% YoY) and capital goods (4.7% YoY). In contrast, output of
durable consumer goods and energy-related products declined (down by 7.0% YoY and 0.5%
YoY, respectively).
Production increased in 25 out of 34 subsectors in August. The strongest gains were recorded

in electrical equipment manufacturing (18.7% YoY) as well as machinery and equipment
production (12.8% YoY), reflecting the advanced stage of the investment cycle co-financed by
the National Recovery Plan (NRP). The largest declines were in the production of tobacco
products (-11.7% YoY), textiles (-4.9% YoY), furniture (-2.3% YoY) and motor vehicles (-1.5%
YoY), all of which are particularly exposed to competition from China. Recently, China has

become Poland’s largest importer, ahead of Germany.
Producer price inflation increased
Producer prices (PPI) increased by 4.2% YoY in August (ING: 4.1%; consensus: 3.6%), following
growth of 3.1% YoY in July (revised up from 2.8% YoY). The marked acceleration in producer
price inflation was largely due to a base effect from August last year, when the PPI index fell by
0.5% MoM, compared with a 0.6% MoM increase this year. Prices in the petroleum refining

sector rose strongly for another consecutive month, reflecting higher crude oil prices on global
markets.
Energy crisis pushing PPI higher
PPI, December 2021=100

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 3
Source: GUS, ING.
Stronger investment and softer consumption seen in 2H26
August data suggests that, despite Poland's surprisingly strong economic performance in
2Q26 (with GDP growth of 3.9% YoY), the outlook for the second half of the year warrants
caution. In particular, slowing wage growth and elevated fuel prices could weigh on household
consumption growth, which already decelerated in 2Q. At the same time, the investment cycle

supported by NRP funds is peaking and generating multiplier effects across the economy, and
this could already be seen in 2Q26 corporate investment data.
While we see scope for GDP growth to exceed our current 3.4% forecast for 2026, geopolitical
uncertainty and the protracted energy crisis prevent us from revising that forecast upwards.

## Document 327: Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

16 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
SOURCE: INSTITUTE OF INTERNATIONAL FINANCE, LSEG, MACROBONDCHART
STRATEGIC PRODUCT VULNERABILITY? CHINA LEAST, THEN EUROPE AND THEN THE US
Koen De Leus
“Germany is too dependent on the US for its security, on Russia for its energy and on China for its exports.” That was, in essence, Brookings’
Constanze Stelzenmüller’s diagnosis in June 2022, and it was – and is – valid as well for Europe as a whole.

But does this dependency also apply to technological and industrial products? The European Commission’s EXternal Vulnerability Index
(EXVI) answers that very question, mapping out the EU’s exposure to foreign supply chains.
Relatively good results for the EU
The EU is relatively well positioned, with a score of 0.18 on net-zero technologies and 0.22 on semiconductors. Raw materials appear to be the
weakest point (0.28). Interestingly, compared to its peers, the EU is more vulnerable than China across all three chains (0.13), but significantly

less vulnerable than the US overall (0.22 vs. 0.28). The only sector where the EU score is weaker than the US one is on semi-conductors, and
the difference is not large (0.22 vs 0.19). Like the EU, the US’ vulnerability is biggest in raw material (0.32). This is, among other factors, due to
outsourcing polluting mining starting in the 1990s. Looking at the essential components for the green transition, the EU score on raw materials is
concerning, but the better one on net-zero technologies is more encouraging.

The EU challenge is not its dependencies but its relatively weak global position
Taking a closer look at the two pillars across the different products, it becomes clear that Europe’s problem is mainly related to its global position
(Pillar 2). Dependency (Pillar 1) itself is not the core issue, with much lower scores than the ones for Pillar 2. The EU dependency risk on raw mate-
rials is even comparable to China score (0.24 vs 0.23), and the EU score on semi-conductors (0.13) is the lowest of the other results for the region.

On the other hand, Pillar 2 scores are more negative, illustrating the lack of EU competitiveness in these areas, with scores hovering between 0.29
for net-zero technologies up to a high 0.39 for semiconductors and 0.41 for raw materials. Europe simply does not produce enough of these pro -
ducts at competitive prices, as big European companies in these sectors are limited. Take semiconductors, for example. Imports are not extremely
EXVI is built on two pillars, with two simple questions for each pr oduct: 1/ How dependent is a r egion on a handful of for eign suppliers? 2/ How

globally competitive is it in that pr oduct measured by its imports versus e xports? The result is a single score from 0 (low vulnerability) to 1
(high). A region scores high (vulnerable) when ther e is both a heavy  reliance on others and a weak market position.
Strategic product vulnerability? China least, then Europe and then the US

### Page 2

2

d’un monde

Chart of the Week / 16 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
One trend is undeniable: China consistently enjoys the lowest vulnerability. Decades of strategic plans, as well as subsidies and other practices
to restrict open markets, have enabled it to build up domestic supplies and stocks.
The EU’s multiple responses
Looking ahead, the EU can deploy a number of options in response, from circularity to domestic mining, including international partnerships.

Brussels is aiming to mine or recycle 10% of critical materials by 2030, but there is paradox at play here, as circularity only works once sufficient
volumes have first been mined and used. Therefore, in the coming years, international partnerships will be a priority and, for that matter, the EU
has been very active on that front since the start of the second Trump presidency.
The EU is aware of the challenges that it facesAnd the EXVI indices are particularly relevant for assessing supply chains targeted by major policy

initiatives, such as the Chips Act (strengthening the EU’s semiconductor ecosystem and technological sovereignty), the Net Zero Industry Act
(NZIA) (building domestic manufacturing capacity for clean-tech products such as solar, wind and batteries) and the Critical Raw Materials Act
(CRMA) (securing and diversifying the EU’s supply of critical and strategic raw materials). The challenges have been identified and an action plan
has been drawn up. Now it needs to be implemented on the ground.

## Document 334: The FOMC seeks a timelier return to target (2026-09-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC seeks a timelier return to target (2026-09-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC seeks a timelier return to target
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:05 September 17 2026 Share
The FOMC delivered a 25bp increase in September to limit secondary inflation risks from
energy prices and strength in domestic investment. Members should follow up with a
second 25bp hike in October to give participants confidence inflation will return to target in a
timely manner. November’s election is a risk to this timeline, however.

### Page 2

The FOMC voted to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a mid-point of 3.875% in September, in line
with market expectations. The updated dot plot points to at least one more hike by year end. 8 of the 18
members who submitted forecasts also signalled the risk of an additional hike by end-2027. Arguably
this would come early in the year, if necessary.
Justifying the September decision, both the Committee’s revised forecasts and the commentary given

by Chair Warsh in the press conference focused attention on the risks to the inflation outlook, while
showing strong confidence in underlying economic momentum.
Annual PCE inflation is forecast to slow from 3.7%yr in 2026 to 2.3%yr in 2027, then 2.1%yr in 2028
and 2.0%yr in 2029. Core inflation meanwhile is expected to track down from 3.4%yr in 2026 to 2.5%yr
in 2027, then 2.2%yr in 2028 and 2.0%yr in 2029. These forecasts are little changed since June

despite today’s rate hike and the additional tightening forecast. Implicit here is that the FOMC is acting
against renewed inflation risks and believes policy will prove effective in time.
The fervent belief the Committee has in the underlying health of the US economy is clear in the GDP
and unemployment rate projections. Despite tighter policy, members’ GDP forecasts were a touch
higher in September than June, at 2.3%yr in 2026, 2.4%yr in 2027, 2.2%yr in 2028 and 2.1%yr in 2029

– all above the FOMC’s current estimate of potential, 2.0%yr.
The unemployment rate is consequently forecast to hold at 4.1% through to the end of 2029, consistent
with full employment being maintained. Like the other median projections, the range for the
unemployment rate is tight, with the upper end of the range just 0.2ppts higher at 4.3%.
The financial context given for today’s decision is also notable. Asked whether the current stance is
restrictive, Chair Warsh stated that, ahead of the decision, both he and the Committee were “hard

pressed” to describe financial conditions as restrictive. September’s hike was then characterised as
removing a dose of accommodation, implying policy is now, at most, neutral.
In gauging the degree of tightening required from here though, Chair Warsh commented that he did not
believe the Committee needed to do “harm” to the labour market to bring inflation down. This suggests
a definitively restrictive stance is likely not necessary. Rather, tightening twice by year end (including

today’s hike) and continuing to flag a willingness to do more should prevent secondary effects from
energy prices and the AI infrastructure buildout, until the inflationary impulse from each factor ebbs.
‘Neutral’ today is therefore likely to be higher than neutral in 2028, 2029 and the longer run, a trend
evident in the median expectation for the fed funds which falls to 3.2% beyond 2029.
Westpac is more cautious on the downside risks to the labour market and growth, but only at the

margin. We meanwhile believe it will take time for headline annual inflation to slow given geopolitical
uncertainty and the limited interest rate sensitivity of domestic US inflation, particularly shelter. Another
hike by year end should give market participants confidence that policy will prove effective within the
forecast window. The Committee is likely to stand ready to deliver a third hike, but this is less than a
50/50 chance currently. Rate cuts will not be seen until 2028 though, and they are likely to only reverse

the current tightening, with limited spare capacity in the US economy outside of tech to bias inflation
above 2.0%yr in the medium term.

## Document 357: US SECTORS CALL_ Energy, Information Technology & Health Care

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Energy, Information Technology & Health Care.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Energy, Information Technology & Health Care
Date:    5 hours ago

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL: Energy,
Information Technology &
Health Care
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 13 Sept 2026
View in browser
Photo by Patrick Hendry / Unsplash

### Page 2

Rising bond yields and oil prices weighed on stocks last week, though a
mostly in-line CPI report on Friday sparked a rebound. The S&P 500 still
fell 0.8%, with nine of the 11 sectors declining. Energy (OW) led, rising
2.0%, and it tops the sector derby at 44.5% ytd. Communication Services
(MW) was the only other gainer, up 1.1%. Health Care (OW) fell the most
at 3.6%. Information Technology (MW) was nearly flat at -0.2% and is up
23.2% ytd, with fundamentals that continue to improve.

Let's take a closer look at Energy, Information Technology, and Health
Care:
(1) Energy. We have recommended overweighting Energy as a hedge
against geopolitical risk. Brent fell below $90 a barrel in late August as
diplomacy briefly eased tensions in the Persian Gulf. Last week, the US
and Iran exchanged strikes, and Houthi attacks set Saudi oil facilities on
fire. Brent futures rose to $104.61 on Friday (chart). The S&P 500 Energy
index is at a record high.

### Page 3

Leading the S&P 500 Energy sector's 44.5% gain so far this year is the Oil
& Gas Refining & Marketing industry, up 127.5% (chart).
The S&P 500 Energy sector remains cheap and under-owned. It currently
accounts for just 3.5% of the S&P 500 market capitalization and 4.7% of
the index's forward earnings share (chart). It's easy to overweight it.

### Page 4

Earnings have driven the Energy sector's rally. Forward earnings is up
57.0% ytd, while the forward P/E is down 7.9%.
The sector trades at 14.5 times forward earnings against 19.1 for the S&P
500 (chart). If the war continues into the November midterm elections, as
President Trump said on Wednesday, Energy should continue to
outperform. We are retaining our overweight rating on Energy.

### Page 5

(2) Information Technology. The AI spending boom is showing up in
revenues, not just backlogs. Oracle beat estimates on Thursday. Total
revenues rose 30% y/y, up from 21% last quarter, as the company's
backlog boosts revenue. Cloud infrastructure revenue rose 121% to $7.4
billion. The stock opened 7.3% higher on Friday, then gave it all back to
close down 1.7%. Investors initially liked the revenue numbers. They are
still deciding what to pay for them.

The Information Technology sector's revenue outlook continues to
improve. Estimates call for IT revenues to grow 33.1% this year and
31.1% next year. Yet the sector trades at 20.2 times forward earnings,
down from 26.5 at the end of 2025.
As of last week, the sector's forward revenues and forward earnings were
up 88.0% and 54.0% y/y, respectively.

### Page 6

The AI buildout needs more than chips. It needs hardware and equipment
to process and store information and also to build more capacity.
Technology Hardware, Storage & Peripherals led the IT sector this week,
rising 3.3%, and is up 35.5% ytd (chart).
Samsung said in July that the memory shortage should run into 2028.
Chipmakers can close that gap only by building capacity, and that means
buying equipment. Consensus estimates call for revenue growth of 22.4%

this year and 36.6% in 2027. Earnings growth follows, from 35.1% to
48.5% (chart). The industry is the sector's best performer ytd, up 69.4%,
yet its forward P/E has fallen to 29.4 from 52.3 on June 30. Earnings are
rising faster than stock prices in this industry.

### Page 7

(3) Health Care. Health Care was the worst-performing sector last week,
falling 3.6%. Every industry declined. Equipment dropped 5.3%,
Biotechnology 4.5%, Pharmaceuticals 3.4%, and Managed Health Care
2.5%. Health Care Services held up best, down 1.8% (chart).
The selloff obscures a sector where earnings are improving. Managed
Care is the clearest case. Its earnings fell 35.0% in 2025. Analysts have
marked up this year's estimate to 11.7% growth, from -1.6% as recently as

March (chart). They see 14.9% next year. Forward earnings has climbed
to $186.00 per share from a low of $152.44, and the forward profit margin
has risen to 3.0% from 2.5% in March. The industry is up 21.0% ytd, the
best in the sector.

### Page 8

Pharmaceuticals are on the same path, a year behind. Consensus shows
earnings falling 3.6% this year, then growing 26.9% in 2027 (chart). The
industry's forward earnings is at a record high.
The S&P 600 Health Care index is up 20.2% ytd, and the S&P 400 is up
17.7%, against 6.6% for the S&P 500 sector (chart). We are retaining our
overweight rating on Health Care.

況仍受荷姆茲海峽 、紅海運輸及攻擊事件影響 ，下

繞道延布 +富查伊拉港 ，依高盛及運輸單位推估流

《天然氣價格：美 持平或略低於 去年，歐洲偏上行 》
美國鑽井活動回升後持平 ，增加較多在天然氣 ，但
外輸能力受限使庫存逐月增加 ，隨美國新的液化天
然氣出口工廠逐漸上線 ，將逐漸改善管道瓶頸 ，七
月庫存雖高於五年均值5.2%，但低於七月的6.7%。
1H26美液化天然氣 (LNG)出口量平均為 174億立方
英尺/日，YOY+23%，預估2H26與上半年量相同 ，
1H27再增加，預估美國4Q26天然氣價格將季節性
走升，持平或略低於去年。卡達能源LNG船已開始
準備出口 ，但目的地非歐洲 ，歐洲儲氣量雖拉升 ，

但僅達 67%，遠低於過往 ，TTF價格大漲吸引運往
亞洲的LNG轉往歐洲，預期4Q26 TTF價格仍有上行
風險 ，但若荷姆茲海峽運力回升速度快於預期 ，
TTF價格將回落。

2
《成品油供應緊張，有利維持高煉油利潤》
受煉油廠關廠 ，煉油產能因俄烏 、中東戰事受損 ，
成品油供應比原油更緊張 ，美國汽、柴油庫存年比
減少，估計全球煉油廠開工率年減 700萬桶/日以上，
遠高於過去區間 ，在供應減少下 ，柴油利潤保持強

勁，美國煉油廠產能利用率已拉高到 98%高檔，雖
因4Q26汽油淡季使獲利季比下滑，但年比仍大幅成
長，其下行風險在於川普要求煉油商降價。
《4Q26能源類股較看好煉油廠》
預估美綜合油企 4Q26受惠油價高於預期 、LNG出
口增加，柴油高裂解價差 ，但進入汽油需求淡季 ，
獲利季比下滑 ，但年比仍大幅成長 ，現金流仍強大 ，
維持資本紀律及股東回報 。但在消息面上 ，受成品
油較原油供應吃緊，4Q26煉油商柴油價差仍佳，較
看好煉油商。

因油價上漲，有利成本較低的美國 PE出口報價由跌
轉漲，獲利可能略為上修 ，呈短多。就塗料，其股
價與利率呈負相關 ，利率升股價下跌 ，雖受惠定價
力、室內裝修與新成屋交屋塗裝 ，但因氣候影響戶
外漆銷售及 DIY市場疲軟 ，Q4獲利較 Q3下滑 ，但
YOY仍成長。水資源處理商維持銷售成長及調漲價
格轉嫁成本上揚 。工業氣體受惠定價力及合約保護 ，

《預期金價短期承壓，但中期仍看升》
近月金價歷經美國就業數據好壞參半 、美伊衝突情
勢升級，推升升息預期，美國計畫加大回購美國長
期國債規模 ，但低於市場預期 ，長期公債殖利率上
升，美元走弱等多空影響 ，本月金價在 4300-4600
美元間震盪 。目前美伊衝突由軍事增加經濟制裁 ，
川普認為期中選舉後可結束伊朗戰爭 ，變數仍多 ，
預期通膨放緩變慢 ，至明年才有較大回落 。但本行
研判應該不會升息 ，若美伊衝突帶來的不利因素漸
放緩，FED轉鴿派或市場縮減升息轉降息預期；受
擔憂債務可持續性 、分散交易對手風險及多元化資
產配置，中國人行加大購金步伐 ，據調查45%受訪
央行認為將未來一年內 將增加黃金儲備 ，年底可望
進入黃金需求旺季，維持金價中期上行。

## Document 398: 【淘金週報】短期金價受升息影響，呈現區間震盪

Source PDF: all report pdf/【淘金週報】短期金價受升息影響，呈現區間震盪.pdf
Report source: CTBC
Date: 2026-09-18
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

0
*新臺幣黃金存摺賣出牌價(包含開盤、盤中及收盤價)均以本行價格為
準，收盤價以本行2026/9/17營業時間最後一盤(約15:30)為主。國際現
貨金價之資料來源為彭博社，資料期間為2026/9/11~2026/9/17。
近五個交易日價格變化暨下週趨勢觀察

【市場短評】
受油價上漲及美國升息預期使金價回跌 ，但在正式升息一
碼及利率點陣圖顯示年底前還有一碼升息空間 ，長債利率
走弱，油價也因外交努力中走弱 ，使本週國際金價止跌轉
漲0.5%。下周關注美國製造業 PMI、消費者信心 、初請失
業金人數及油價表現 ，本行認為此次為風險管理式升息 ，
基準情境認為年底升息一碼後 ，聯準會將進入按兵不動觀
察期，2027年中後可望轉向寬鬆立場 。金價已部分定價今
年升息預期，使金價短期在4000~4500美元間區間震盪 ，
但擔憂美國財政風險 、債務可持續性 、分散交易對手風險
及多元化資產配置 ，中國人行購金量加大 ，建議黃金回檔
後分批承接，維持金價中期上行。

## Document 401: 【產業聚焦】算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：ＣＰＯ進入量產驗證期

Source PDF: all report pdf/【產業聚焦】算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：ＣＰＯ進入量產驗證期.pdf
Report source: CTBC
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：
CPO 進入量產驗證期
產業聚焦
2026/09/17
• 短期商用確定：1.6T 模組放量、
交換器 CPO 落地
• 中期瓶頸突破：異質封裝良率
與可維護性成焦點
• 長期潛力可期：光學 I/O 仍需
等待 Tape-out 認證
AI叢集擴張，網路效率決定GPU利用率
大型模型訓練由數千顆GPU擴張至數萬甚至更大規模後，系統瓶頸已由單顆晶片效能延伸至Scale-up、
Scale-out與跨資料中心互連，交換器頻寬、傳輸延遲、每位元功耗及故障率若未同步改善，GPU會因等
待資料而閒置，新增資本支出也難以轉化為有效Token產出。市場衡量標準因此由GPU數量，進一步轉向
每瓦、每埠及每個Rack可穩定傳輸的資料量，光通訊價值也隨節點數、網路層數與連線密度同步上升。
雲端客戶的採購重點將更偏向有效算力、網路uptime與總體擁有成本，而非只比較名目GPU規模。
可插拔與 CPO 並行，2026 年迎商用量產拐點
TrendForce估計AI光模組市場由2025年165億美元增至2026年260億美元，800G以上出貨占比逾六成，

短期收入仍由800G／1.6T模組、EML／CW雷射、DSP與FAU領先。另一方面，NVIDIA Spectrum-X
Ethernet Photonics已進入量產，Broadcom Tomahawk 6大量出貨並以Davisson推進102.4T CPO，聯
電亦完成SILITH 1.6T矽光子晶圓首批交付。產業已跨過技術展示階段，但CPO仍會先集中於高Radix交換
器。可插拔、NPO與CPO將依距離、功耗、維修及供應鏈控制需求並行，收入不會在單一年度完成替代，
2026至2027年的主旋律將是分層導入成熟Scale-out技術。
量產與毛利率驗證優先，光I/O保留長線彈性
SEMICON Taiwan 2026把焦點由PIC效能推進至晶圓製程、異質封裝、主動對位、多層測試與現場維修
，顯示競爭核心轉為能否以可控成本大量製造。選股宜先檢查平台與客戶、量產時點、收入占比、良率、
毛利率及資本支出回收，再評估2030年TAM。2028年前後若Optical I/O進入XPU封裝，ASIC設計、光

子Chiplet、先進封裝及Rack整合可望取得更高價值量，但仍須等待Tape-out、標準與系統驗證。台灣供
應鏈的核心觀察值應由合作公告轉為晶圓出貨、封裝一次通過率、Rack部署與毛利率變化。
CPO在AI資料中心的滲透率持續提升

### Page 2

AI萬卡叢集瓶頸：網路效率決定有效算力
Scale-up與Scale-out承擔不同任務
Scale-up連接同一運算域內的GPU／XPU，主要交換梯度、參數與記憶體資料，追求極低延遲
、高雙向頻寬與高Radix，現階段由NVLink及短距離銅互連主導，UALink生態持續推進。
Scale-out則跨Rack或跨叢集擴張節點，重視可擴充性、可靠度與距離，主要採Ethernet或
InfiniBand，已直接帶動800G與1.6T光模組。Scale-across再把不同資料中心連成訓練與推論
Fabric，對長距離光連結、同步與容錯提出更高要求。其中集合通訊對尾端延遲特別敏感，任一
鏈路壅塞或重傳都會同步拖慢運算域；Scale-out則更在意東西向流量、超額訂閱比與故障切換。
光學價值量隨節點、層數與連線密度增加
當Tier-1 Switch由Rack內移至End-of-Row，且單通道由100G升至200G，PCB走線、連接器與
銅纜損耗快速提高，系統必須加入DSP、Retimer及低損耗材料。電光轉換位置因此由面板模組

移向Switch ASIC，形成LPO、NPO與CPO，下一階段再以Optical I/O延伸多Rack Scale-up。
價值鏈不再侷限於光模組，並擴大至ASIC、矽光子PIC、雷射、FAU、先進封裝、測試、系統組
裝與液冷。採購決策也從單一元件規格轉向端到端鏈路預算、每瓦頻寬、可維修性與總持有成本；
能同時提供電、光、封裝與系統驗證資料的供應商將具備更高競爭門檻。
【注意事項】本行所提供之資訊僅供參考用途。本行當盡力提供正確之資訊，所載資料均來自或本諸我們相信可靠之來

### Page 3

埠速與架構分流：可插拔守量，CPO攻高頻寬
埠速升級：800G維持主流，1.6T進入量產導入期
800G以8×100G/lane為主，供應鏈與客戶驗證相對成熟，1.6T進入8×200G/lane，隨IEEE
P802.3dj與OIF規範推進，已成為2026年AI資料中心升級重點，3.2T預計朝8×400G/lane發展
，仍需突破SerDes、DSP、光源、散熱與面板密度限制，埠速代表單一介面的傳輸能力，並不
直接等同CPO滲透率，短期營收仍會先反映在可插拔模組與關鍵光電元件。不同CSP亦會依交換
器週期、銅纜距離與功耗預算錯開升級，因此同一年度仍將並存多種埠速；供應鏈應以design-
in、認證完成與量產坡度判斷實質貢獻。
架構遷移：光靠近晶片後，價值轉向封裝與系統
LPO移除模組內DSP以降低功耗與延遲，保留可插拔維修性，但通道容忍度較低，NPO把光引擎
置於ASIC鄰近區域，在電氣距離與模組化之間折衷，CPO將光引擎與Switch ASIC整合於同一封
裝，優先解決高Radix交換器的功耗與面板密度，Optical I/O再把光子Chiplet導入XPU封裝，用

於多Rack Scale-up與記憶體解耦。四種方案的量產時點、供應鏈與毛利結構不同，評價不宜混
在同一TAM。投資上最先可驗證的是量產BOM、出貨顆數與良率，封裝內光I/O則需另看Tape-
out、標準與客戶共同設計。

需求增量來自埠速與連線密
度同步提升
AI Cluster擴張不只提高單埠速度，也
增加GPU節點、Leaf／Spine交換器層
數與每顆加速器對應的光連線數，因而
光學需求呈現量與價同步上升，最先受
惠環節包括800G／1.6T模組、EML／
CW雷射、DSP、Driver／TIA與FAU光
纖陣列。需求能見度仍受GPU交付、交
換器建置及網路拓樸架構影響。
CPO開始進入特定商用量產，不會立即
排擠可插拔模組，反而先擴大整體光學
市場價值量，兩者將於不同架構層級長
期共存。供應鏈評估仍應清楚區分既有
量產收入、客戶認證中的新品，以及尚
未形成標準採購規格的長期方案。追蹤
時宜同步比對總埠數、每顆GPU光連結
數、單通道ASP與生產良率之變化。

CPO商用起步：2026量產、2028–2029加速
CPO由低基期進入商用
曲線，2028年後加速
TrendForce估計AI資料中心CPO滲
透率由2026年約0.55%升至2030年
35.74%，增量主要集中於2028至
2029年Scale-up導入，CPO／
NPO市場規模則可由2025年約1億
美元增至2030年逾390億美元。由
於早期滲透率高度受少數美系雲端
龍頭與運算平台牽動，單一大型客
戶的驗證時程或拉貨節奏調整，即
可實質改變全年的成長斜率。
低基期與平台量產同時出現，代表
2026年是商用起點而非獲利高峰，
近期應先觀察Switch出貨、每台光
引擎數量、良率與客戶部署，而非
把2030年TAM提前一次反映。只有
Field Return 與維修機制通過驗證
後，規模化滲透才具可持續性。

5
架構分工由距離、功耗與維
修成本共同決定
可插拔模組具標準成熟、可快速熱插拔
更換與多供應商生態優勢，1.6T乃至未
來3.2T仍會在主流Scale-out網路層維
持龐大出貨規模；NPO有效縮短電氣距
離，同時保留部分模組化彈性；CPO優
先用於高Radix、超高功耗及面板密度
受限的旗艦級Switch；Optical I/O則鎖
定跨Rack Scale-up運算域與XPU封裝
內部互連。
四條路線更可能長期分工，而非單一路
線全面取代。滲透率斜率將取決於功耗、
系統良率、維修標準與總持有成本。採
購決策還受故障域（Blast Radius）影
響：可插拔可局部更換，CPO若維修恐
需停用整張交換主板，節能紅利極易被

備品儲備與停機損失抵銷，使現場可維
護性成為放量前提。
四類光學架構的量產分工

200G通道縮短銅線距離，CPO降低功耗
電氣損耗降至約4 dB
傳統可插拔架構讓 200Gb/s 訊號由
Switch ASIC 穿越基板封裝、PCB
走線、面板連接器與 Cage 模組後
才進入光收發器。NVIDIA 指出，此
完整電氣路徑的損耗最高可達 22
dB，系統必須在板上加入大量 DSP、
Retimer 與超低損耗板材以維護訊
號完整性，進而推高板層數與散熱
成本。當通道進一步朝 400G/lane
推進時，高頻插損與串擾的設計裕
度已逼近極限。CPO將光引擎移至
Switch ASIC鄰近封裝，將高頻電氣
損耗大幅降至約 4 dB，因而成為
51.2T 至 102.4T 以上高 Radix 交換
器的重要演進方向。未來銅線將退
回封裝基板內或極短距板內傳輸，
跨板與跨機櫃鏈路將逐步提高光連
結占比，封裝內與極短距板內仍將
由電氣互連主導。
200G電氣路徑損耗由22 dB降至約4 dB
資料來源：NVIDIA ；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

6
光路分光結合外置雷射，介
面功耗降至 9W
NVIDIA 資料顯示，傳統 1.6T 可插拔光
模組介面功耗約達 30W（DSP 20W +
EML 10W），而 CPO 架構可將介面功
耗驟降至約 9W（光引擎 7W + 外置雷
射源 2W），此一大幅節能效益，除來
自省去高速 SerDes 驅動長走線的電氣
功耗外，關鍵更在於光路架構的革新：
CPO 採用外置連續波（CW）雷射，透
過矽光子晶片內部分光機制，將每
1.6Tb 頻寬所需的雷射數量由傳統可插
拔的 8 顆 EML 大幅縮減至 2 顆外置光
源，在降低功耗的同時亦將熱源移出交
換器核心。對百萬瓦級 AI Factory 而言，
網路用電下降能直接釋放更多電力與冷

卻空間供 GPU 運算，但系統端也必須
納入外置雷射 ELSFP 壽命、可拆式光纖
連接與現場維修（Field Return）機制，
以確保整體 TCO 真正獲得優化。
光互連功耗由約30W降至約9W
資料來源：NVIDIA；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

400G逼近極限：純矽、TFLN與InP共存
3.2T／204.8T轉向400G
通道
下一代3.2T光模組與204.8T交換器
將朝400G/lane推進，元件須同時
提高調變頻寬、降低VπL與插入損
耗，並維持雷射效率、熱穩定與晶
圓均勻度。隨PAM4接近頻寬極限，
驅動器線性度、接收靈敏度與FEC延
遲也將影響鏈路預算。
Broadcom已展示400G/lane
Taurus DSP搭配400G EML／PD，
先支援低功耗1.6T並建立3.2T基礎；
聯電與SILITH則規劃把純矽MZM延
伸至400G/lane。速度提升不只考
驗單顆元件，也考驗PIC製程、封裝、
光源與測試共同收斂，量產觀察值
應包括頻寬、功耗、良率與客戶驗
證週期。若實驗室性能無法在溫度
循環與晶圓分布下維持，則量產良
率與交期將面臨挑戰。
光靠近晶片後，頻寬密度提高、能耗下降

7
純矽、TFLN與InP依效能及
成熟度分工
純矽MZM具CMOS相容、12吋晶圓與
成熟供應鏈優勢，適合規模化與成本控
制，但高速效率與驅動電壓仍需優化；
TFLN具高頻寬、低損耗與較佳調變效
率，聯電、SILITH及HyperLight正推進
量產平台，封裝與材料均勻度是關鍵；
InP可整合高效率雷射、光放大器
（SOA）與高速調變器，適合主動光元
件，成本、熱與良率門檻較高。
材料平台絕非單一勝者全拿的零和競爭，
最終取決於系統規格、封裝整合成本與
能否導入一線客戶長約供應鏈。實務上
將朝異質整合（Heterogeneous
Integration）演進：由矽基承擔被動
波導與大規模封裝整合，TFLN負責高

速調變，InP則專注提供高效雷射光源
與訊號放大，發揮各自最佳物理特性。
400G/lane材料平台比較
資料來源：Broadcom、UMC、Soitec；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

SEMICON Taiwan 2026：矽光子轉入量產競賽
矽光子論壇議程由元件效能延伸至完整光子Fabric
SEMICON Taiwan 2026矽光子國際論壇以Die-to-Die至Rack-to-Rack為主題。Cisco、Marvell
與Lightmatter先討論Scale-up、Scale-out及Scale-across架構，說明網路已成為影響XPU利用
率與每瓦Token產出的系統資源；台積電以COUPE說明EIC與PIC透過SoIC整合，聯電把
200G/lane延伸至400G/lane與TFLN，日月光、imec及Soitec則聚焦光電異質整合。議程把設
計、製程與系統放在同一條量產鏈上，反映CPO已進入工程整合階段。
真正稀缺能力轉為可重複製造流程
Lam、Onto、ficonTEC、Advantest與Enli把討論推進至薄膜、折射率、側壁形貌、晶圓篩選、
雙面電光測試、主動對位與翹曲補償。產業問題已由「光能否取代部分銅線」，轉為「晶圓、封
裝、光纖耦合與系統能否在可控成本下大量生產」。2026年後的競爭指標不只Tb/s，也包括

Known Good Die、整體良率、測試時間、外置雷射可靠度與現場維修性。能把多個環節串成穩
定流程的供應商，更可能先取得收入與毛利率。設備商雖可在終端CPO出貨前取得工程線訂單，
但收入可能隨驗收而波動，真正可持續的供應商須累積製程資料庫、縮短對位與測試秒數。

異質封裝拉長製程：KGD與良率相乘成關鍵
EIC、PIC、ASIC與光纖
共同封裝放大失效率
傳統光模組可以獨立測試與更換，
CPO則把Switch ASIC、EIC、PIC、
雷射介面、透鏡、FAU與大尺寸基
板納入同一系統，任何一顆光引擎
或一條纖維通道失效，都可能造成
高價值組件報廢，整體良率也會受
到多個子系統良率相乘影響。當單
一封裝含多顆光引擎時，即使各子
系統良率看似良好，整體良率亦會
因乘數效應而大幅滑落。
ASE的典型流程涵蓋PIC／EIC後製、
晶圓或裸晶鍵合、光學組裝、基板
整合及最終系統測試，橫跨電性、
光學、熱與機械公差，量產管理難
度高於單一元件，製造端還須建立
逐站追溯，把晶圓座標、鍵合批次
與最終通道表現連結。
CPO流程橫跨晶圓、CoW、光學與系統組裝

9
先測再組與Known Good
Die決定成本結構
量產策略必須在高價值ASIC封裝前剔除
壞Die，並在光引擎組裝前後重複確認
電性與光損耗。晶圓級篩選可降低後段
報廢，主動對位與原位校準決定光纖耦
合效率，翹曲與熱應力控制影響最終一
次通過率；外置雷射與可拆式纖維則關
係到現場維修時間。若測試秒數過長，
良率提升也可能被設備折舊與在製品占
用抵銷；若coverage不足，則會導致
高昂的客退與返工成本。
供應鏈評價應從單顆元件規格，延伸至
良率爬升速度、UPH、返工率、測試成
本與Field Return。只有這些關鍵指標
同步改善，CPO才具備擴張至更多埠數
的經濟性。能以數據閉環把缺陷回饋至
晶圓與組裝製程的廠商，才能在良率爬
升曲線中脫穎而出。

### Page 10

設備商搶先受惠：雙面電光量測與對位前置
晶圓至系統須建立多層電光測試
矽光子雖常採45／65nm等成熟線寬，光學效能卻高度依賴薄膜厚度、折射率、波導側壁、應力
與材料均勻度，製程控制並不簡單。Onto與Enli等方案把薄膜、形貌與光損耗映射前移至晶圓或
裸晶階段，先識別異常區域；ficonTEC強調重組晶圓、裸晶與模組需導入雙面電光量測、原位校
準、翹曲補償及自動對位；Advantest則推進可擴充、標準化的NPO／CPO測試架構。設備投資
因此早於最終出貨放量，量測資料若能與晶圓座標、封裝批次及系統序號連結，便可建立由缺陷
來源至Field Return的追溯閉環，以統計製程控制縮小規格分布。
商業化關鍵指標由標稱頻寬轉向UPH與Field Return
測試覆蓋率不足會把壞Die帶入昂貴封裝，覆蓋過度又會拉長測試秒數並降低UPH，量產必須在
成本與風險間取平衡，應追蹤晶圓與光引擎良率、主動對位精度、整機一次通過率、每顆測試時
間、ELSFP壽命、維修工時與Field Return，相關機會將擴散至薄膜量測、光譜影像、探針與治

具、主動對位、自動化組裝及Burn-in，若標準遲未收斂或客戶採垂直整合，設備需求也可能呈
現專案化與收入波動，設備訂單通常由實驗室、工程線、複製線再走向量產線，與終端營收可能
相隔數季，評價時應區分單機開發、批量複製、耗材治具及維護服務。

光學I/O入XPU封裝：2028年前先看NRE
光子Chiplet延伸Scale-
up距離
光學I/O把電光轉換由交換器旁移入
GPU、XPU或ASIC封裝，透過UCIe
等晶粒介面連接光子Chiplet，可望
在較低pJ/bit下提供數十至逾
100Tb/s的封裝級頻寬，並支援記
憶體解耦與跨機櫃Scale-up，其經
濟性來自以少量光纖取代大量高速
電氣I/O，釋放封裝邊緣與主板走線
資源。
Intel已展示整合式OCI光子晶粒，
Ayar Labs則以TeraPHY推進CW-
WDM光學介面與UCIe連結。相較
Switch CPO，光學I/O更接近運算
晶片與封裝共同設計，單機價值量
與技術門檻都更高。不過光源供應、
封裝熱耦合、記憶體一致性與軟體
拓樸仍須同步驗證。
Intel OCI光子Chiplet整合至運算封裝

開放標準決定CPO能否由單一平台擴大採購
Open CPO建立Rack系
統藍圖
Lightmatter與19家公司於2026年8
月在OCP啟動CPO系統架構倡議，
發布約300頁基礎白皮書，目標建立
符合MHS與Open Rack v3的共同藍
圖，使Scale-up域由72顆擴張至
1,024顆以上節點，並支援十萬顆
XPU叢集。共同藍圖可降低每家
CSP自行定義接口的重工。
隨SerDes通道速率逼近448G，銅
線極限傳輸距離驟減至數十公分以
內，促使標準化範圍由單顆光引擎
封裝，進一步擴大至交換器、XPU
運算板、外置光源、光纖理線、高
功率散熱、供電及模組化維修介面。
對台廠供應鏈而言，跨廠商互通性
測試（Interop）與現場可維護性將
成為新一代量產認證的核心門檻。
台灣系統與光元件業者已加入Rack級驗證

14
規格收斂決定第二波滲透率
OIF以3.2T CPO及200G/lane介面推進
光引擎、外置雷射、連接器與散熱；
OCI MSA希望讓不同XPU、Switch與
光學供應商互通；IEEE 802.3dj則建立
200G/lane Ethernet實體層基礎。多套
規格並行有助擴大供應來源，也可能造
成接口分歧與認證重工。對台廠而言，
標準化雖可擴大可服務市場，也會提高
價格透明度與第二來源壓力。
2026至2027年間，關鍵觀察指標聚焦
於各標準規格凍結時程、跨廠商互通性
測試ELSFP外置光源模組壽命及可拆式
光纖連接器之量產驗證。待產業標準全
面收斂後，CPO採購模式方能由封閉的
垂直整合旗艦平台，順利擴展至多供應

商的公有雲市場採購體系。能穩定交付
驗證樣品並累積大量互通數據實績之業
者，方能在規格定案放量後率先取得首
波規模化採購訂單。
四套開放規格的分工與進度

台灣供應鏈依營收時點分層，量產證據最關鍵
2026至2027年先看已有平台與出貨實績的環節
台積電以COUPE、SoIC與CoWoS切入光電整合，聯電與SILITH已完成12吋1.6T PIC首批量產交
付，日月光投控／SPIL具備CPO封裝、組裝與測試布局，鴻海與FIT則由光纖互連延伸至Switch
及Rack系統。波若威等被動光元件廠的機會來自FAU、Fiber Shuffle與可拆式連接，但仍須確認
客戶認證及CPO收入占比。這些公司具較明確平台位置，評價重點應落在出貨量、良率、營收占
比與毛利率，晶圓代工與OSAT雖較早受惠前置開發，獲利仍要看量產批次是否持續，系統與光
纖廠較接近終端部署，認列則受機房建置與驗收影響。
2027至2028年再看ASIC、Rack與Optical I/O驗證
創意與Lightmatter等合作推進CPO ASIC，世芯-KY布局跨Rack Scale-up，緯穎展示Optical
I/O Compute Tray，設備與測試商則受惠晶圓篩選、雙面電光量測、主動對位及Burn-in需求。

此層的單案價值量與獲利彈性較高，收入時點也更依賴Tape-out、封裝驗證、客戶認證與標準
收斂。投資排序須區分量產、系統驗證與合作公告。設備商可能由工程線先行受惠，但若規格反
覆或客戶垂直整合，訂單也可能停留在少量專案，須再追蹤同機型複製台數、耗材維護及第二廠
區導入。

### Page 16

產業方向確立，投資回歸出貨、良率與毛利率
量產鏈優先，OpticalI/O保留長線選擇權
部分Switch CPO已進入量產部署，產業整體仍處量產爬坡、良率改善與系統驗證並行，Optical
I/O則仍以NRE、Tape-out及平台驗證為主，2026年最確定的收入仍是800G／1.6T可插拔模組
與關鍵雷射，NVIDIA與Broadcom量產則確認Switch CPO形成第二條成長曲線。台灣晶圓代工、
封裝測試、光纖互連與系統整合具直接位置，設備商可在良率爬升前取得需求。Optical I/O進入
XPU封裝後價值量更高，較可能於2028年前後具規模，應等待Tape-out、封裝與客戶認證。投
資順序可依可見度分成三層：已量產光模組與光源、進入平台部署的Switch CPO，以及仍待
Tape-out的Optical I/O，前兩層重視出貨進度與實質獲利貢獻。
催化劑與風險須同步追蹤
近期催化劑包括Vera Rubin／Spectrum-X出貨、1.6T放量、Tomahawk 6與Davisson部署、

SILITH晶圓交付、COUPE／CoWoS共同設計、OCP首批規格及400G/lane元件驗證。主要風險
則為1.6T／3.2T可插拔延壽、CPO整體良率與外置雷射可靠度低於預期、標準分歧、客戶提高自
製比例，以及估值領先收入。整體產業展望維持正向，選股仍應依序檢查客戶與平台、量產時點、
收入占比、良率、毛利率及資本支出回收。若高速可插拔持續降功耗、CPO維修成本偏高，導入
斜率可能低於預期，反之，規格收斂、第二客戶複製與Field Return下降，將推動導入斜率加速
超越預期。

力強さを欠く中国の消費振興策
中国政府は消費特化型の 5 カ年計画を初めて策定した。もっとも、消費者の節約志向の根源にある不動産
不況や若年層を中心とした雇用不安の早期回復は見込みにくく、目標達成は難航すると予想される。
■サービ
ス消費を重点項目の筆頭に位置づけ
中国政府は7月、「消費拡大に関する第15次 5カ
年計画」を策定した。消費に特化した5 カ年計画を
打ち出すのは、今回が初めてである。計画の全体目標
は、広範なサービス消費を含め、経済全体に占める家
計消費の比重を拡大することである。目的は、中国経
済の成長モデルの転換である。 これまで中国経済は投
資に重点を置いた成長をしてきたが、GDP に占める
家計消費の割合は 4 割弱にとどまり、欧州主要国や
日本の 5～6割、米国の 7割弱と比べて小さい。 一方、
不動産不況や過剰生産能力の問題が深刻化するなか、
従来の投資偏重型の成長は限界を迎えており、消費主

導の成長モデルの実現が急務となっている。
本計画における具体的な数値目標としては、社会消費品小売総額（商品小売＋飲食サービス）
を 2030年までに約 60 兆元に引き上げることが設定された（右上図）。目標達成には、5 年間の
平均で＋3.7％の消費拡大が必要となる。なお、前段で述べた全体目標と異なり、数値目標には飲
食サービス以外のサービス消費は含まれていない。
消費拡大に向けて取り組みを推進する重点項目として、①サービス消費の質的向上、②財消費
の拡大と高度化、③新たな消費の形態・モデル・シーンの育成、④消費能力の向上、⑤消費環境
の大幅改善、⑥消費促進の制度やメカニズムの整備、の六つが掲げられた。筆頭に位置づけられ
ているサービス消費については、銀髪経済（シルバーエコノミー）の拡大や保育サービスの利用
促進、文化・観光消費の発展、健康消費やスポーツ消費の奨励、教育・研修サービスの向上とい
った政策方針が挙げられている。7月末に開催された共産党中央政治局会議においても、サービス
消費の潜在力を活用することの必要性が強調されており、中国政府の消費振興策の重点がモノ消

費からコト消費に変化している様子がうかがえる。
■節約志向が浸透しているサービス消費
政府は計画策定にあわせ、幅広いサービス消
費の動向を捉えた新指標である「社会消費商
品・サービス小売総額」の公表を6 月から開始
した。新指標には、旧来の小売売上高（社会消
費品小売総額）で捕捉されていた飲食サービス
に加え、通信・情報サービス、観光・文化、ス
ポーツ・レジャーなど、幅広いサービス消費が
含まれている。新旧指標の最近の動きを比べる
と、旧指標は2024年以降伸び率が継続的に低下
研究員 古宮 大夢
(komiya.hiromu@jri.co.jp）

3. 新たな消費の形態・モデル・シーンの育成
4. 消費能⼒の向上

### Page 3

ASIA
MONTHLY 2026 年 09 月（No.306）
JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
2
している一方で、新指標は底堅い推移となってい
る（前頁右下図）。
このように、サービス消費全体としては、財消
費に比べて堅調な伸びを示しているものの、消費
全体のけん引役にはなりがたい状況である。サー
ビス消費の内訳をみると、新旧指標の乖離が拡大
した 2025 年以降の景気減速局面において、節約
志向の高まりから支出内容に濃淡が生じている
（右上図）。医療・保健や交通・通信、教育など
の削減が困難な支出項目は可処分所得の伸びを
超えて増加する傾向が見られる一方、飲食や家事
代行など家庭内で代替可能なサービスは支出が
停滞している。また、賃料などの住居費も、不動
産不況を背景に低迷している。さらに、政府が消
費拡大の柱に据えている観光消費も、経済的な理
由から「安近短（費用が安く、距離が近く、日程
が短い）」な旅行が重視されており、海外ではな
く国内旅行に人気が集まっているほか、国内旅行

者の1人当たり支出額も減少している （右下図）。
■消費低迷構造の打開策を欠き、目標達成は困難
そのほかの重点項目では、③新たな消費の形
態・モデル・シーンの育成において、人工知能と消費を融合させたデジタル消費の発展や、環境
に配慮したグリーン消費の促進、地域の実情に合わせた「首発経済」などが挙げられ、一定の需
要創出は見込まれる。首発経済とは、企業が新製品の発表、新業態や新技術・新サービスの導入、
および海外・国内ブランドの1 号店（首店）のオープンなど、すべての「初（はじめて）」をキ
ーワードにした経済活動の総称である。
もっとも、消費の本格回復は期待薄である。消費者の節約志向の根源にあるのは、不動産不況
による逆資産効果と若年層を中心とする軟調な雇用環境である。各都市で住宅購入要件の緩和な
どが実施されているものの、購入層の減少といった構造的な要因が住宅販売の重石になっている。
新築住宅価格に加え、市場の実勢を反映しやすい中古住宅価格も下落が続いているほか、在庫の
回転期間も長期化していると見込まれ、住宅市場の本格回復にはなお数年を要する見通しである。

雇用環境についても、本年入り後の年齢階級別の失業率をみると、16～24歳と 30～59歳が 2025
年以前の高い水準で推移しているほか、25～29歳は昨年を上回って悪化しており、改善の兆しが
見られない。不動産不況を受けて2022年以降「悲観」が続いている消費者マインドは、本年入り
後に一段と悪化している。
政府は 5 カ年計画のなかで、質の高い十分な雇用環境や手ごろな価格の住宅供給の最適化、不
動産市場の健全化を含めた複数の経路による財産所得の増加などに言及しているものの、具体的
な方針は示していない。また、人工知能の発展が労働代替につながり、家計の将来不安が一段と
拡大して、逆説的に消費者マインドを悪化させる恐れもある。5カ年計画では消費低迷の根本原因
へのアプローチが不十分であるため、消費主導型経済への転換は難航が予想される。
